   2|                                                               SPECIAL TOWN MEETING JANUARY 7, 2014
   3|                        The Moderator declared a quorum present and called the meeting to order at 7:02 PM in the Lunenburg
   4|                       High School Auditorium. Due to the large turnout a recess was called to allow voters to check in. Meeting
   5|                       resumed at 7:26 p.m. The Moderator led us in the Pledge of Allegiance and reminded attendees of the
   6|                       rules of procedure.
   7|                        ARTICLE 1. To see if the Town will vote to appropriate, borrow or transfer from available funds, a sum
   8|                       of money to be expended under the direction of the School Building Committee for construction of a new
   9|                        Lunenburg Middle/High School, designed for approximately 820 students in Grades 6 - 12, to be located
  10|                       at 1079 Massachusetts Avenue, Lunenburg, MA 01462, which school facility shall have an anticipated
  11|                       useful life as an educational facility for the instruction of school children of at least 50 years, and for
  12|                       which the Town may be eligible for a school construction grant from the Massachusetts School Building
  13|                        Authority ("MSBA"). The MSBA's grant program is a non-entitlement, discretionary program based on
  14|                       need, as determined by the MSBA, and any project costs the Town incurs in excess of any grant approved
  15|                       by and received from the MSBA shall be the sole responsibility of the Town. Any grant that the Town of
  16|                       Lunenburg may receive from the MSBA for the Project shall not exceed the lesser of (1) fifty-nine point
  17|                       eleven percent (59.11%) of eligible, approved project costs, as determined by the MSBA, or (2) the total
  18|                       maximum grant amount determined by the MSBA. Board of Selectmen recommends approval, 5-0.
  19|                       Finance Committee recommends approval, 5-1. VOTED (2/3rds declared) to appropriate by borrowing
  20|                       $72,975,321.00 and to meet this appropriation the Board of Selectmen and Town Treasurer are authorized
  21|                       to borrow said sum under M.G.L. chapter 44, or pursuant to any other enabling authority. The Town of
  22|                       Lunenburg acknowledges that the MSBA's grant program is a non-entitlement, discretionary program
  23|                       based on need, as determined by the MSBA, and any project costs the Town of Lunenburg incurs in
  24|                       excess of any grant approved by and received from the MSBA shall be the sole responsibility of the Town
  25|                       of Lunenburg; provided further that any grant that the Town of Lunenburg may receive from the MSBA
  26|                       for the Project shall not exceed the lesser of (1) fifty-nine point eleven percent (59.11%) of eligible,
  27|                       approved project costs, as determined by the MSBA, or (2) the total maximum grant amount determined
  28|                       by the MSBA; provided that any appropriation hereunder shall be subject to and contingent upon an
  29|                       affirmative vote of the Town to exempt the amounts required for the payment of interest and principal on
  30|                       said borrowing from the limitations on taxes imposed by M.G.L. 59, Section 21C (Proposition 2 ½); and
  31|                       that the amount of borrowing authorized pursuant to this vote shall be reduced by any grant amount set
  32|                       forth in the Project Funding Agreement that may be executed between the Town of Lunenburg and the
  33|                        MSBA.
  34|                        ARTICLE 2. To see if the Town will vote to amend the Town's Zoning Bylaw by adding a new Section
  35|                       9.0, entitled Temporary Moratorium on Medical Marijuana Treatment Centers/Registered Marijuana
  36|                        Dispensaries, that would provide as follows, and further to amend the Table of Contents to add Section
  37|                       9.0, "Temporary Moratorium on Medical Marijuana Treatment Centers/Registered Marijuana
  38|                        Dispensaries:"
  39|                       Section 9.0.1. Purpose
  40|                        By vote at the State election on November 6, 2012, the voters of the commonwealth approved a law
  41|                       regulating the cultivation, distribution, possession and use of marijuana for medical purposes. The law
  42|                       provides that it is effective on January 1, 2013. On May 8, 2013, the State Department of Public Health
  43|                       promulgated Regulations that became effective on May 24, 2013. Currently under the Zoning Bylaw, a
  44|                        Medical Marijuana Treatment Center, identified in the State Regulations as a Registered Marijuana
  45|                        Dispensary, is not a permitted use in the Town of Lunenburg. The State Regulations provide guidance to
  46|                       the Town in regulating medical marijuana, including Medical Marijuana Treatment Centers/Registered
  47|                                                                                              133

===PAGE 134=== (ocr, 35 lines)
   1|             Dispensary, is not a permitted use in the Town of Lunenburg. The State Regulations provide
   2|             guidance to the Town in regulating medical marijuana, including Medical Marijuana Treatment
   3|             Centers/Registered Marijuana Dispensaries. The regulation of medical marijuana raises novel
   4|            and complex legal, planning, and public safety issues and the Town needs time to study and
   5|             consider the regulation of such use and address such novel and complex issues, as well as to
   6|            address the potential impact of the State regulations on local zoning and to undertake a planning
   7|             process to consider amending the Zoning Bylaw regarding regulation of medical marijuana
   8|             treatment centers and other uses related to the regulation of medical marijuana. The Town
   9|            intends to adopt a temporary moratorium on the use of land and structures in the Town for
  10|             Medical Marijuana Treatment Center/Registered Marijuana Dispensaries so as to allow the Town
  11|            sufficient time to engage in a planning process to address the effects of such structures and uses
  12|            in the Town and to enact bylaws in a manner consistent with sound land use planning goals and
  13|             objectives.
  14|             Section 9.0.2. Definition
  15|            "Medical Marijuana Treatment Center" shall mean a not-for-profit entity, as defined by
  16|             Massachusetts law only, registered by the Department of Public Health as a Registered
  17|             Marijuana Dispensary, that acquires, cultivates, possesses, processes (including development of
  18|            related products such as food, tinctures, acrosols, oils or ointments), transfers, transports, sells,
  19|             distributes, dispenses, or administers marijuana, products containing marijuana, related supplies,
  20|             or educational materials to qualifying patients or their personal caregivers.
  21|             Section 9.0.3. Temporary Moratorium
  22|             For the reasons set forth above and notwithstanding any other provisions of the Zoning Bylaw to
  23|            the contrary, the Town hereby adopts a temporary moratorium on the use of land or structures for
  24|            a Medical Marijuana Treatment Center/Registered Marijuana Dispensary. The moratorium shall
  25|             be in effect through December 31, 2014 or until such time as the Town adopts Zoning Bylaw
  26|            amendments that regulate Medical Marijuana Treatment Centers/Registered Marijuana
  27|             Dispensaries and related uses, whichever occurs earlier. During the moratorium period, the
  28|             Town shall undertake a planning process to address the potential impacts of medical marijuana in
  29|            the Town, consider the State Regulations and related uses, and shall consider adopting new
  30|             Zoning Bylaws to address the impact and operation of Medical Marijuana Treatment
  31|             Centers/Registered Marijuana Dispensaries and related uses; or take any action relative thereto.
  32|             Board of Selectmen recommends disapproval. Finance Committee - no recommendation.
  33|             Planning Board recommends approval 5-1. ARTICLE 2 was DEFEATED
  34|             Special Town Meeting adjourned at 9:30 P.M.

  27|                        ARTICLE 26. To see if the Town will vote to amend Article XXV of the Town Bylaws, entitled Public
  28|                        Access Cable Committee, by deleting the existing bylaw and replacing it with the following; or take any
  29|                       other action relative thereto. [Submitted by the Board of Selectmen] Board of Selectmen recommends
  30|                       approval, 5 - O. Finance Committee recommends approval, 6-0. VOTED UNAIMOUSLY
  31|                        Article 1. Establishment
  32|                       The name of the organization shall be the Public Access Cable Committee, herein referred to as PACC.
  33|                       The Selectmen activated and appointed the PACC in July 2007 to assist with the development of
  34|                        Lunenburg TV Channels 8 & 9.
  35|                        Article 2. Mission Statement
  36|                       The Committee was created for the purpose of establishing a local access TV Station operating on cable
  37|                       for the Town of Lunenburg. PACC is to oversee programming in areas of public affairs, education,
  38|                       entertainment and town government. The PACC shall be responsible for the formulation and
  39|                       promulgation of policy directives and guidelines for the operation of the PACC; provided, however, that
  40|                                                                                              143

===PAGE 144=== (ocr, 35 lines)
   1|                                                                 TOWN MEETING EXCERPTS:
   2|                                                        ANNUAL TOWN MEETING MAY 3, 2014
   3|            nothing in this section shall be construed to authorize any member of the PACC, nor a majority of such
   4|            members, to become involved in the day-to-day administration of the Lunenburg Public Access.
   5|            Article 3. Section intentionally deleted.
   6|            Article 4. Membership
   7|            Section 1. Number of Members. PACC shall consist of five- (5) members who shall be appointed by the
   8|            Lunenburg Board of Selectmen from residents of the Town of Lunenburg or full time students at
   9|            Lunenburg High School.
  10|            Section 2. Term of Appointment. The term shall be for three-(3) years or, in the case of vacancy, any
  11|            portion of an unexpired term, so arranged that the term of office as nearly an equal number of members as
  12|           is possible shall expire each year. The above Term of Appointment shall be effective with the
  13|            appointments by the Board of Selectmen commencing in fiscal year 2009.
  14|            Section 4. Composition of Membership. Members shall reflect the makeup of the community at large.
  15|            Any resident of the town or any Lunenburg High School student is eligible to submit his/ her name to the
  16|            Lunenburg Selectmen for consideration to be appointed to PACC.
  17|            Section 5. Voting Power. All duly appointed current members of PACC shall have the right to vote on
  18|            all issues.
  19|            Section 6. Resignation. Members appointed by the Lunenburg Board of Selectmen shall resign in
  20|            writing to the Board of Selectmen and PACC.
  21|            Section 7. Attendance. Regular meeting attendance is expected for all members of PACC. In the event
  22|            of three- (3) consecutive absences, except for the reason of health or extenuating circumstances as duly
  23|            reported to the Chair in advance of the meeting, PACC may request resignation of that member through
  24|            the appointing authority.
  25|            Section 8. Vacancies. Vacancies shall be filled by the Board of Selectmen.
  26|            Section 9. Representations. No member of PACC shall make written or oral presentations for PACC
  27|            unless authorized by a vote of PACC.
  28|            Section 10. Non-PACC Members. PACC may appoint a Non-PACC member to act on PACC's behalf.
  29|            In such a case, all written materials, oral presentations or communications from the Non-PACC member
  30|            on behalf of PACC, will need to be approved by a vote of PACC prior to his/ her presentation or
  31|             execution.
  32|            Article 5. Meetings
  33|            Section 1. Regular Meetings. Regular meetings will be held as determined by the PACC with the time
  34|            and place to be posted at Town Hall.
  35|                                                                                              144

===PAGE 145=== (ocr, 38 lines)
   1|                                                                            TOWN MEETING EXCERPTS:
   2|                                                                    ANNUAL TOWN MEETING MAY 3, 2014
   3|                       Section 2. Special Meetings. The Chair or a majority of PACC members may call for Special Meetings.
   4|                       Section 3. Organizational Meeting. The organizational meeting shall be held at the regularly scheduled
   5|                        monthly meeting after the start of each fiscal year.
   6|                       Section 4. Open Meeting Law. All meetings shall be posted and conducted in compliance with the
   7|                       provisions of Chapter 39, Sections 23, 23A and 23B. The meeting agenda shall be posted in accordance
   8|                       with the Massachusetts Open Meetings Law and all other applicable Town Bylaws.
   9|                       Section 5. Quorum. The presence of a simple majority of the total membership, as defined in Article 4,
  10|                       Section 1 shall be necessary and sufficient to constitute a quorum.
  11|                        Article 6. Officers.
  12|                       Section 1. Officers. The officers shall consist of a Chairman, Vice Chairman and Secretary as elected by
  13|                        the members.
  14|                       Section 2. Election. Election for officers shall take place at the PACC organizational meeting.
  15|                       Section 3. Terms of Office. Term of office for the officers shall be for one- (1) year, with no restrictions
  16|                        on re-election.
  17|                       Section 4. Vacancy. Any vacancy may be filled at a regular or special meeting of PACC. It shall be for
  18|                       the unexpired term of the previous incumbent officer. In the case of the Chair, the Vice-Chair shall fill
  19|                       the position of the Chair for the remainder of the unexpired term of the Chair.
  20|                       Section 5. Duties of Officers. Chair- the Chair shall be the chief executive officer and be subject to the
  21|                       direction of PACC. The Chair or his/ her designee shall provide the agendas for meetings, preside at
  22|                       meeting and orient new members. The Chair or his/ her designee shall provide all PACC members a copy
  23|                       of the Open Meeting Law as required by state statute. Vice-Chair- the Vice-Chair shall exercise all the
  24|                       functions of the Chair, during the absence or disability of the Chair, and when so acting, shall have all the
  25|                       powers and be subject to all restrictions of the Chair. Secretary- the Secretary shall take the meeting
  26|                       minutes, post notices of upcoming meetings and provide minutes to PACC members. He/ She shall post
  27|                       the meeting agenda in accordance with the Massachusetts Open Meetings Law and all other applicable
  28|                        Town Bylaws.
  29|                        Article 7. Amendments. The PACC may consider amendments or alterations to these by-Laws at either a
  30|                       regular or special PACC meeting. In either case, notification of the meeting and its purpose shall be
  31|                        given fourteen- (14) days prior to the assembly. The notice shall include a summary of the proposed
  32|                        amendment, the full text of the proposed amendment or alteration, and a statement of the purpose of the
  33|                       proposed change. The proposed amendment must be approved by a majority vote of PACC. It will then
  34|                       be sent to the Lunenburg Board of Selectmen for approval, and if so approved, for insertion in the warrant
  35|                       and be submitted to the next Town Meeting for approval. This protocol applies only to the PACC's
  36|                       ability to initiate changes to these bylaws, and is not intended to usurp the rights given to registered voters
  37|                       of the Town specified in MGL Chapter 39, Section 10.
  38|                                                                                              145

===PAGE 146=== (ocr, 34 lines)
   1|                                                                 TOWN MEETING EXCERPTS:
   2|                                                        ANNUAL TOWN MEETING MAY 3, 2014
   3|            Article 8. Staff. The Town Manager shall appoint or hire staff as is necessary, to meet programming,
   4|            content and/ or operational requirements. The Town Manager will consult with the PACC and Program
   5|            Director for their input prior to making such appointments.
   6|            Article 9. Other Provisions.
   7|            Section 1. Establishment of Policy and Procedures. PACC may establish policy or procedure according
   8|            to the following guidelines: Such proposals must be presented to the PACC members at one meeting and
   9|            voted on at the following meeting, except when the waiting period is waived by a majority vote of the
  10|            members. The approved policy or procedure shall be included in the PACC Policy Manual.
  11|            Section 2. Possible Conflict. If any part of these Bylaws are in conflict with Town Bylaws, those of the
  12|            Town shall have preference.
  13|            Section 3. Annual Budget. PACC will submit an annual budget to the Town Manager for inclusion in the
  14|            Annual Town Meeting Warrant. It shall be due when all other Town Department budgets are required to
  15|            be submitted to the Town Manager either by request and/ or Charter requirement. Said budget may be
  16|            fully debated, discussed and amended at the Annual Town Meeting. VOTED UNANIMOUSLY
  17|            ARTICLE 27. To see if the Town will vote to amend the Zoning Bylaw, Section 4.0, Use Regulations,
  18|            by adding a new Section 4.16 entitled Registered Marijuana Dispensaries, or take any action relative
  19|            thereto. [Submitted by the Planning Board] Board of Selectmen recommend approval. Finance
  20|            Committee no direct financial impact.
  21|            4.16.1. Purpose
  22|            To provide for the placement of Registered Marijuana Dispensary (RMDs), in accordance with the
  23|            Humanitarian Medical Use of Marijuana Act, G.L. c. 94C App. 1-1 - Chapter 369 of the Acts of 2012 in
  24|            locations suitable for lawful medical marijuana facilities and to minimize adverse impact of RMDs on
  25|            adjacent properties, residential neighborhoods, historic districts, schools, playgrounds and other locations
  26|            where minors congregate by regulating the siting, design, placement, security and removal of RMDs.
  27|            4.16.2. Applicability
  28|            4.16.2.1. All RMDs shall comply with The Regulations promulgated by the Massachusetts Department of
  29|            Public Health (DPH) 105 CMR 725.000 Implementation of an Act for the Humanitarian Medical Use of
  30|            Marijuana, effective May 24, 2013, and any subsequent amendments thereto.
  31|            4.16.2.2. The Town reserves the right to require provisions in the DPH Regulations for which the state
  32|            granted waivers and/or exemptions to the RMD applicant based on the provisions of Section 8.3.3. (as to
  33|            special permit) and Section 8.4. (as to development plan review).
  34|                                                                                              146

===PAGE 147=== (ocr, 36 lines)
   1|                                                                            TOWN MEETING EXCERPTS:
   2|                                                                    ANNUAL TOWN MEETING MAY 3, 2014
   3|                       4.16.3. Definition
   4|                       Registered Marijuana Dispensary: also known as a Medical Marijuana Treatment Center, means a not-for-
   5|                       profit entity registered under 105 CMR 725.000, that acquires, cultivates, possesses, processes (including
   6|                       development of related products such as edible marijuana-infused products, tinctures, aerosols, oils, or
   7|                       ointments), transfers, transports, sells, distributes, dispenses, or administers marijuana, products
   8|                       containing marijuana, related supplies, or educational materials to registered qualifying patients or their
   9|                       personal caregivers. Unless otherwise specified, RMD refers to the site(s) of dispensing, cultivation, and
  10|                        preparation of marijuana.
  11|                       4.16.4. Location
  12|                       4.16.4.1.                RMD's that include retail, processing and cultivation are allowed in Commercial Districts
  13|                       (C) by special permit.
  14|                       4.16.4.2.                Cultivation and/or agricultural processing: Allowed in all Districts by right if applicant is
  15|                        eligible for protection under Section 4.1.3(b) and G.L. c. 40A, § 3. Any cultivation within a building will
  16|                       require a special permit.
  17|                       4.16.4.3.                RMD facilities that cultivate and process, but do not do retail of marijuana, are allowed in
  18|                        Office Park and Industrial Districts (OP/I) by special permit.
  19|                       Section 4.16.5. Procedure for Submittal
  20|                       4.16.5.1. The Planning Board shall be the Special Permit Granting Authority (SPGA) for a RMD Special
  21|                       Permit per MA Gen. Law 40A, Section 9. Siting shall be by Special Permit and Development Plan
  22|                        Review per Section 8.4 of the Lunenburg Protective Bylaw.
  23|                       4.16.5.1.a)              Criteria In granting any special permit, the Planning Board shall assure that the proposed
  24|                       use:
  25|                                                            Will not be injurious or dangerous to the public health or unduly hazardous
  26|                       because of traffic congestion, danger or fire or explosion or other reasons.
  27|                                                2)          Will not have a material adverse effect on the value of land and buildings in the
  28|                       neighborhood or on the amenities of the neighborhood.
  29|                                                3)          Will be operated with reasonable regard for order and sightliness, if an open use.
  30|                                                            Will not produce noise, vibration, smoke, dust, odor, heat or glare observable at
  31|                       the lot lines in amounts clearly detrimental to the normal use of adjacent property.
  32|                       4.16.5.2. It is recommended that a concept plan showing the proposed use be submitted to the Planning
  33|                        Board for review and discussion prior to the filing for Development Plan Review.
  34|                       4.16.5.3. The provisions of Section 8.3.3. (as to Special Permits) and Section 8.4. (as to Development
  35|                        Plan Review) shall apply.
  36|                                                                                              147

===PAGE 148=== (ocr, 36 lines)
   1|                                                                 TOWN MEETING EXCERPTS:
   2|                                                        ANNUAL TOWN MEETING MAY 3, 2014
   3|            4.16.5.4. Filing under Section 8.3.3. and 8.4. may be simultaneous.
   4|            4.16.5.5. A special permit granted under this Section shall have a term limited to the duration of the
   5|            applicant's ownership of the premises as a RMD. A special permit may be transferred only with the
   6|            approval of the Special Permit Granting Authority in the form of an amendment to the special permit with
   7|            all information required in this Section.
   8|            4.16.6. Conditional Standards
   9|            4.16.6.1 Distance All proposed RMDs shall be sited accordingly as stated in Massachusetts Department
  10|            of Public Health (DPH) 105 CMR 725.110 (A) (14), which states a RMD shall not be sited within a
  11|            radius of five hundred (500) feet of a school, daycare center, or any facility in which children commonly
  12|            congregate. The 500 foot distance under this section is measured in a straight line from the nearest point
  13|            of the facility in question to the nearest point of the proposed RMD.
  14|            4.16.6.2 Setbacks and Buffer Strips Cultivation facilities located outside of Retail Commercial (RC),
  15|            Commercial (C), or Office Park and Industrial Districts (OP/I) shall be surrounded by a buffer strip which
  16|            shall be two hundred (200) feet in depth unless the applicant can demonstrate, and the Planning Board
  17|            finds, that adequate buffering can be provided in a narrower buffer strip. In all other districts existing
  18|            setbacks will apply.
  19|            4.16.6.3 Design Standards In addition to requirements contained elsewhere in this Bylaw, Section 4.6.5.
  20|            Design Standards shall be applicable to a RMD. The applicant shall meet all requirements of the
  21|            Commercial District (C) or DPH guidelines, whichever is more restrictive.
  22|            4.16.6.4 Waivers The Planning Board may waive any of the conditional standards within this section
  23|            provided that such waiver will not derogate from the intent established herein.
  24|            4.16.7. Security
  25|            4.16.9.1. All security measures to be approved by the Lunenburg Fire and Police Chiefs. An active
  26|            security system shall be required for all RMD locations and approved by both the Lunenburg Fire and
  27|            Police Chiefs and submitted to the Planning Board
  28|            4.16.9.2. RMDs shall be open to inspection by the Fire Department, Police Department, Building Official
  29|            and the Board of Health with twenty-four (24) hours' notice of request for such inspection to be made by
  30|            the Town Department or Official. A property contact shall be available to such Town Department or
  31|            Official twenty-four hours a day, seven days a week.
  32|            4.16.10. Documentation
  33|            The Planning Board shall be provided with all decisions or approvals, denials or other substantive actions
  34|            by DPH regarding the RMD and all submittals of information between the applicant or RMD and DPH.
  35|            VOTED UNANIMOUSLY

===PAGE 149=== (ocr, 34 lines)
   1|                                                                            TOWN MEETING EXCERPTS:
   2|                                                                    ANNUAL TOWN MEETING MAY 3, 2014
   3|                        ARTICLE 28. To see if the Town will vote to amend the General Bylaws by inserting a new Section
   4|                        XXXI, entitled Right to Farm Bylaw, or take any action relative thereto. [Submitted by the Agricultural
   5|                        Commission] Board of Selectmen recommend approval 5-0. Finance Committee no direct financial
   6|                       impact.
   7|                       Section 1. Legislative Purpose and Intent. The purpose and intent of the Bylaw is to state with emphasis
   8|                       the Right to Farm accorded to all citizens of the Commonwealth under Article 97, of the Constitution, and
   9|                       all state statutes and regulations thereunder including but not limited to Massachusetts General Laws,
  10|                       Chapter 40A, Section 3, Paragraph 1; Chapter 90, Section 9; Chapter 11, Section 125A and Chapter 128,
  11|                       Section 1A. We the citizens of Lunenburg restate and republish these rights pursuant to the Town's
  12|                       authority conferred by Article 89 of the Articles of Amendment of the Massachusetts Constitution,
  13|                       ("Home Rule Amendment"). This General Bylaw encourages the pursuit of agriculture, promotes
  14|                       agriculture-based economic opportunities, and protects farmlands within the Town of Lunenburg by
  15|                       allowing agricultural uses and related activities to function with minimal conflict with abutters and local
  16|                       agencies. This Bylaw shall apply to all jurisdictional areas within the Town.
  17|                       Section 2. Definitions. The word "farm" shall include any parcel or contiguous parcel of land, or water
  18|                        bodies used for the primary purpose of agriculture, or accessory thereto. The words "farming" or
  19|                       "agriculture" or their derivatives shall include, but not be limited to the following:
  20|                              a. Farming in all its branches and the cultivation and tillage of the soil
  21|                              b. Dairying
  22|                              c. Production, cultivation, growing and harvesting of any agricultural, aquacultural, floricultural,
  23|                                    viticultural or horticultural commodities
  24|                              d. Growing and harvesting of forest products upon forest land and any other forestry or lumbering
  25|                                    operations
  26|                              e. Keeping and raising of livestock, horses, poultry, swine, cattle, sheep ratites (such as emus,
  27|                                    ostriches, rheas) and camelids (such as llamas and camels) and other domesticated animals for
  28|                                    food and other agricultural purpose, including bees and fur-bearing animals.
  29|                             "Farming" shall encompass activities including, but not limited to the following:
  30|                              Operation and Transportation of slow-moving farm equipment over roads within the Town;
  31|                              Control of pests, including, but not limited to, insects, weeds, predators and disease organisms of
  32|                              plants and animals;
  33|                              Application of manure, fertilizers and pesticides;
  34|                                                                                              149

===PAGE 150=== (ocr, 40 lines)
   1|                                                                 TOWN MEETING EXCERPTS:
   2|                                                        ANNUAL TOWN MEETING MAY 3, 2014
   3|                  Conducting agricultural related educational and farm based recreational activities, including agri-
   4|                  tourism, provided that the activities are related to marketing the agricultural output or services of the
   5|                  farm;
   6|                  Processing and packaging of the agricultural output of the farm and the operations of a Farmer's
   7|                  Market or Farm Stand including signage thereto;
   8|                  Maintenance, repair, or storage of seasonal equipment or apparatus owned or leased by the farm
   9|                  owner or manager used expressly for the purpose of propagation, processing, management or sale of
  10|                  the agricultural products; and
  11|                  On farm relocation of earth and the clearing of ground for farming operations.
  12|                  Section 3. Right-to-Farm Declaration. The Right-to-Farm is hereby recognized to exist within the
  13|                  town of Lunenburg. The above described agricultural activities may occur (of)on holidays, weekdays
  14|                  and weekends by night or day and shall include the incidental noise, odors, dust and fumes associated
  15|                  with normally accepted agricultural practices. It is hereby determined that whatever impact may be
  16|                  caused to others through the normal practice of agriculture is more than offset by the benefits of
  17|                  farming to the neighborhood, community and society in general. The benefits and protections of the
  18|                  Bylaw are intended to apply (exclusively) to those (commercial) agricultural and farming operations
  19|                  and activities conducted in accordance with generally accepted agricultural practices. Moreover,
  20|                  nothing in this Right-to-Farm Bylaw shall be deemed as acquiring any interest in land, or as imposing
  21|                  any land use regulation, which is properly the subject of state statute, regulation or local zoning law.
  22|                  Section 4. Disclosure Notification. In order to ensure that prospective owners and prospective
  23|                  tenants are aware of the policy of the Town of Lunenburg expressed in this Bylaw regarding
  24|                  agricultural uses and practices, the following notification of this policy shall prominently posted in
  25|                  the Town Hall within 30 days of this Bylaw becoming effective. Copies will also be made available
  26|                  for distribution. "This notification is to inform persons and entities who are about to acquire or lease
  27|                  rental property within the Town of Lunenburg that it is the policy of the Town of Lunenburg to
  28|                  conserve, protect and encourage the maintenance and improvement of the agricultural land, for the
  29|                  production of food and other agricultural products and for the preservation of its natural and
  30|                  ecological value. This notification is to further inform such owners and entities that farming
  31|                  activities, including the raising and keeping of livestock, take place in the Town of Lunenburg and
  32|                  that such activities may cause or create noise, (duct) dust and odors which adversely impact or are
  33|                  incompatible with the use or enjoyment of the property within the Town, including the property about
  34|                  to be acquired or leased."
  35|                  Property owners should make efforts to inform prospective tenants or buyers that Lunenburg is a
  36|                  Right-to-Farm community. In addition, the notification language required by this section shall appear
  37|                  annually in the Town's Annual Report.
  38|                  Section 5. Resolution of Disputes. Any person who seeks to complain about the operation of a farm
  39|                  may, notwithstanding pursing any other available remedy, file a grievance with the Select Board, the
  40|                                                                                              150

===PAGE 151=== (ocr, 30 lines)
   1|                                                                            TOWN MEETING EXCERPTS:
   2|                                                                    ANNUAL TOWN MEETING MAY 3, 2014
   3|                             Zoning Enforcement Officer, or the Board of Health, depending on the nature of the grievance. The
   4|                             filing of the grievance does not suspend the time within which to pursue any other available remedies
   5|                             that the aggrieved may have. The Zoning Enforcement Officer or Select Board shall forward a copy
   6|                             of the grievance to the Agricultural Commission or its agent, which shall review and facilitate the
   7|                             resolution of the grievance, and report its recommendations to the referring Town authority within an
   8|                             agreed upon time frame. The Board of Health, except in case of imminent danger or public health
   9|                             risk, may forward a copy of the grievance to the Agricultural Commission or its agent, which shall
  10|                             review and facilitate the resolution of the grievance, and report its recommendations to the Board of
  11|                             Health within an agreed upon time frame.
  12|                              Section 6. Severability Clause. If any part of the Bylaw is for any reason held to be unconstitutional
  13|                             or invalid, such decision shall not affect the remainder of the Bylaw. The Town of Lunenburg hereby
  14|                              declares the provisions of this Bylaw to be severable. VOTED UNANIMOUSLY (with amendments
  15|                             as noted with strikethroughs and italics.)

  19|                       and Dillis Civil Design Group, Inc.," and voted by the Town of Lunenburg Planning Board on March 10,
  20|                       2014, and to authorize the Board of Selectmen to acquire by gift, deed and eminent domain the land,

===PAGE 161=== (ocr, 47 lines)
   1|                                                                                 THE 2014 BALLOT QUESTIONS
   2|                         QUESTION 1: Law Proposed by Initiative Petition
   3|                         This proposed law would eliminate the requirement that the state's gasoline tax, which was 24 cents per gallon as of September
   4|                         2013, (1) be adjusted every year by the percentage change in the Consumer Price Index over the preceding year, but (2) not be
   5|                         adjusted below 21.5 cents per gallon.
   6|                         WHAT YOUR VOTE WILL DO
   7|                         A YES VOTE would eliminate the requirement that the state's gas tax be adjusted annually based on the Consumer Price Index.
   8|                         A NO VOTE would make no change in the laws regarding the gas tax.
   9|                                                              PREC A                 PREC B                   PREC C                 PREC D                            TOTAL
  10|                         YES                                        698                    539                     635                     626                            2498
  11|                         NO                                         456                    395                     399                     401                            1651
  12|                         BLANKS                                     33                                              19                      25                              98
  13|                         TOTAL                                    1187                     955                    1053                   1052                            4247
  14|                         QUESTION 2: Law Proposed by Initiative Petition
  15|                         This proposed law would expand the state's beverage container deposit law, also known as the Bottle Bill, to require deposits on
  16|                         containers for all non-alcoholic non-carbonated drinks in liquid form intended for human consumption, except beverages primarily
  17|                         derived from dairy products, infant formula, and FDA approved medicines. The proposed law would not cover containers made
  18|                          of paper-based biodegradable material and aseptic multi-material packages such as juice boxes or pouches. The proposed law
  19|                         would require the state Secretary of Energy and Environmental Affairs (EEA) to adjust the container deposit amount every five
  20|                         years to reflect (to the nearest whole cent) changes in the consumer price index, but the value could not be set below five cents.
  21|                         The proposed law would increase the minimum handling fee that beverage distributors must pay dealers for each properly
  22|                         returned empty beverage container, which was 21/4 cents as of September 2013, to 31/2 cents. It would also increase the
  23|                         minimum handling fee that bottlers must pay distributors and dealers for each properly returned reusable beverage container,
  24|                         which was 1 cent as of September 2013, to 31/2 cents. The Secretary of EEA would review the fee amounts every five years
  25|                          and make appropriate adjustments to reflect changes in the consumer price index as well as changes in the costs incurred by
  26|                         redemption centers. The proposed law defines a redemption center as any business whose primary purpose is the redemption
  27|                         of beverage containers and that is not ancillary to any other business. The proposed law would direct the Secretary of EEA to
  28|                          issue regulations allowing small dealers to seek exemptions from accepting empty deposit containers. The proposed law would
  29|                         define small dealers as any person or business, including the operator of a vending machine, who sells beverages in beverage
  30|                         containers to consumers, with a contiguous retail space of 3,000 square feet or less, excluding office and stock room space; and
  31|                          fewer than four locations under the same ownership in the Commonwealth. The proposed law would require that the regulations
  32|                         consider at least the health, safety, and convenience of the public, including the distribution of dealers and redemption centers by
  33|                         population or by distance or both. The proposed law would set up a state Clean Environment Fund to receive certain unclaimed
  34|                          container deposits. The Fund would be used, subject to appropriation by the State Legislature, to support programs such as the
  35|                         proper management of solld waste, water resource protection, parkland, urban forestry, air quality and climate protection. The
  36|                         proposed law would allow a dealer, distributor, redemption center or bottler to refuse to accept any beverage container that is not
  37|                          marked as being refundable in Massachusetts. The proposed law would take effect on April 22, 2015.
  38|                         WHAT YOUR VOTE WILL DO
  39|                         A YES VOTE would expand the state's beverage container deposit law to require deposits on containers for all non-alcoholic,
  40|                         non-carbonated drinks with certain exceptions, increase the associated handling fees, and make other changes to the law.
  41|                          A NO VOTE would make no change in the laws regarding beverage container deposits.
  42|                                                              PRECA                  PREC B                   PREC C                 PREC D                            TOTAL
  43|                         YES                                        208                     152                    176                     198                             732
  44|                         NO                                                                792                     872                     848                            3481
  45|                          BLANKS                                                             13                       5                       6
  46|                         TOTAL                                    1187                     955                    1053                   1052                             4247

===PAGE 162=== (ocr, 51 lines)
   1|              QUESTION 3: LAW PROPOSED BY INITIATIVE PETITION
   2|              This proposed law would (1) prohibit the Massachusetts Gaming Commission from issuing any license for a casino or other
   3|              gaming establishment with table games and slot machines, or any license for a gaming establishment with slot machines: (2)
   4|              prohibit any such casino or slots gaming under any such licenses that the Commission might have issued before the proposed
   5|             law took effect; and (3) prohibit wagering on the simulcasting of live greyhound races. The proposed law would change the
   6|              definition of "illegal gaming" under Massachusetts law to include wagering on the simulcasting of live greyhound races, as well as
   7|              table games and slot machines at Commission-licensed casinos, and slot machines at other Commission-licensed gaming
   8|              establishments. This law would make those types of gaming subject to existing state laws providing criminal penalties for, or
   9|              otherwise regulating or prohibiting, activities involving illegal gaming. The proposed law states that if any of its parts were
  10|              declared invalid, the other parts would stay in effect.
  11|              WHAT YOUR VOTE WILL DO
  12|              A YES VOTE would prohibit casinos, any gaming establishment with slot machines, and wagering on simulcast greyhound
  13|              races.
  14|              A NO VOTE would make no change in the current laws regarding gaming.
  15|                                                PREC A                PREC B                   PREC C                 PREC D                            TOTAL
  16|              YES                                     386                     280                     340                     340                           1346
  17|              NO                                       785                     652                    702                     698                           2837
  18|              BLANKS                                     16                     23                      11                      14                              64
  19|              TOTAL                                  1187                     955                   1053                    1052                            4247
  20|              QUESTION 4: LAW PROPOSED BY INITIATIVE PETITION
  21|              This proposed law would entitle employees in Massachusetts to earn and use sick time according to certain conditions.
  22|              Employees who work for employers having eleven or more employees could earn and use up to 40 hours of paid sick time per
  23|              calendar year, while employees working for smaller employers could earn and use up to 40 hours of unpaid sick time per
  24|              calendar year. An employee could use earned sick time if required to miss work in order (1) to care for a physical or mental
  25|              illness, injury or medical condition affecting the employee or the employee's child, spouse, parent, or parent of a spouse; (2) to
  26|              attend routine medical appointments of the employee or the employee's child, spouse, parent, or parent of a spouse; or (3) to
  27|              address the effects of domestic violence on the employee or the employee's dependent child. Employees would earn one hour of
  28|              sick time for every 30 hours worked, and would begin accruing those hours on the date of hire or on July 1, 2015, whichever is
  29|             later. Employees could begin to use earned sick time on the 90th day after hire. The proposed law would cover both private and
  30|              public employers, except that employees of a particular city or town would be covered only if, as required by the state
  31|              constitution, the proposed law were made applicable by local or state legislative vote or by appropriation of sufficient funds to pay
  32|             for the benefit. Earned paid sick time would be compensated at the same hourly rate paid to the employee when the sick time is
  33|              used. Employees could carry over up to 40 hours of unused sick time to the next calendar year, but could not use more than 40
  34|              hours in a calendar year. Employers would not have to pay employees for unused sick time at the end of their employment. If an
  35|              employee missed work for a reason eligible for earned sick time, but agreed with the employer to work the same number of
  36|             hours or shifts in the same or next pay period, the employee would not have to use earned sick time for the missed time, and the
  37|              employer would not have to pay for that missed time. Employers would be prohibited from requiring such an employee to work
  38|              additional hours to make up for missed time, or to find a replacement employee. Employers could require certification of the need
  39|              for sick time if an employee used sick time for more than 24 consecutively scheduled work hours. Employers could not delay the
  40|              taking of or payment for earned sick time because they have not received the certification. Employees would have to make a
  41|              good faith effort to notify the employer in advance if the need for earned sick time is foreseeable. Employers would be prohibited
  42|              from interfering with or retaliating based on an employee's exercise of earned sick time rights, and from retaliating based on an
  43|              employee's support of another employee's exercise of such rights. The proposed law would not override employers' obligations
  44|              under any contract or benefit plan with more generous provisions than those in the proposed law. Employers that have their own
  45|              policies providing as much paid time off, usable for the same purposes and under the same conditions, as the proposed law
  46|              would not be required to provide additional paid sick time. The Attorney General would enforce the proposed law, using the same
  47|              enforcement procedures applicable to other state wage laws, and employees could file suits in court to enforce their earned sick
  48|              time rights. The Attorney General would have to prepare a multilingual notice regarding the right to earned sick time, and
  49|              employers would be required to post the notice in a conspicuous location and to provide a copy to employees. The state
  50|              Executive Office of Health and Human Services, in consultation with the Attorney General, would develop a multilingual outreach
  51|                                                                                              162

===PAGE 163=== (ocr, 28 lines)
   1|                         program to inform the public of the availability of earned sick time. The proposed law would take effect on July 1, 2015, and
   2|                         states that if any of its parts were declared invalid, the other parts would stay in effect.
   3|                         A YES VOTE would entitle employees in Massachusetts to earn and use sick time according to certain conditions.
   4|                         A NO VOTE would make no change in the laws regarding earned sick time.
